# Cross-check ledger: claims, sources and verdicts

Every claim checked against outside sources, with its source, verdict and the
action taken. Open items are in [`CROSSCHECK_PLAN.md`](CROSSCHECK_PLAN.md).
Read this file only to recheck or ground a claim; add a row here for every new
claim taken from secondary literature.

Status: **all tiers (A–E) checked online on 2026-11-14 and the corrections
applied.** The essay was checked on the same day and reviewed again on
2026-11-15. The quotations of the *Tractatus* in the analysis were checked
against the three PDFs in this folder.

## What the PDFs did and did not confirm

| Claim in the analysis | In the PDFs? |
| :-- | :-- |
| German text first appeared in Ostwald's *Annalen der Naturphilosophie* (1921) | Yes: Ogden's editorial note in Gutenberg #5740 |
| Wittgenstein refused Russell's introduction for a Reclam edition (1920); the Ostwald printing carried it in German | No |
| 1914 donation to artists (Rilke, Trakl) via Ficker; fortune to siblings in 1919 | No |
| "hausgewordene Logik" is Hermine Wittgenstein's phrase | No |
| Sraffa anecdote comes from Malcolm / von Wright | No |

## The original plan (2026-11-14, all tiers executed)

### Tier A — factual and historical claims (objective, do first)

1. Title: Moore's suggestion and the allusion to Spinoza.
2. Pinsent: descent from Hume's brother; death in a test flight, May 1918.
3. Russell's introduction: the Reclam refusal and the Ostwald printing.
4. Paris courtroom model: notebook entry of 29.9.1914; the "read it in a magazine" detail.
5. Russell abandoning *Theory of Knowledge* (1913) because of Wittgenstein's criticism.
6. Ficker letter (1919): exact wording about the unwritten part and the ethical point.
7. Tolstoy's *Gospel in Brief* and "the man with the gospels".
8. The 1914 donation and the 1919 transfer of the fortune.
9. Teaching posts, the Hütteldorf gardening job, Haus Wittgenstein dates, the Hermine phrase.
10. Sraffa anecdote: source, setting, "form" vs "multiplicity".
11. 1929: "Some Remarks on Logical Form" and Ramsey's part in the colour-exclusion problem.
12. "Lecture on Ethics": date and the murder example.
13. Ladder precedents: Sextus (*Adv. Math.* VIII 481), Schopenhauer, Mauthner.
14. Lichtenberg's "Es denkt"; Schopenhauer's "eye of the world".
15. Hertz's wording ("innere Scheinbilder oder Symbole"); Kant *Prolegomena* §13; Leibniz *Monadology* §§1–3.
16. *Investigations* §43, §47, §107: wording and section numbers.
17. Who first published truth tables (the analysis says Wittgenstein "introduces" them; Peirce and Post need mentioning).
18. Sheffer stroke: whether "NOR" is the right gloss for Sheffer's 1913 operator.
19. Influence claims: Waismann and Carnap on probability; Tarski and Carnap on metalanguages; Davidson.

### Tier B — interpretive claims (compare with SEP/IEP and named scholars)

1. *Sachverhalt* as a *possible* combination vs an *existing* atomic fact (Ogden, Stenius, Black).
2. 1.11–1.12 as a rejection of Russell's general facts.
3. The nature of simple objects; the 2.0211–2.0212 argument for substance.
4. Names have only *Bedeutung*, propositions only *Sinn* (against Frege).
5. Whether the N operator really yields all of quantified logic (Fogelin, Geach, Soames debate).
6. Elimination of identity (Hintikka's exclusive readings of the variables).
7. Number as exponent of an operation; how far this rejects logicism.
8. Logical probability (5.15) and its relation to Bolzano and Keynes.
9. Solipsism and the metaphysical subject (5.6–5.641).
10. 6.3751 and the 1929 collapse: which claims the 1929 paper actually makes.
11. Traditional vs resolute readings of 6.54: fairness of both summaries, and whether intermediate positions (McGinn, Goldfarb) should be named.
12. "Logic is transcendental" (6.13) and "ethics is transcendental" (6.421): Kantian reading.
13. The characterisation of Mauthner and of the Vienna Circle.

### Tier C — translation and terminology

1. Greek terms against the published Greek translation (Kitsopoulos): *Sachverhalt*, *Sachlage*, *Sinn*, *Bedeutung*, *sinnlos*, *unsinnig*, *Bild*.
2. Contested renderings: 4.128 *zahllos*, 5.1361 *der Aberglaube*, 5.62 *die allein ich verstehe*, 6.13 / 6.421 *transzendental*.
3. Pears–McGuinness quotations (main propositions 1–7) against a second copy.

### Tier D — bibliography

Every entry in sections Β΄–Δ΄ of the bibliography was written from memory:
check author, title, publisher, year and page ranges, and confirm the Greek
translation entry (translator, publisher, year).

### Tier E — figures and text integrity

1. The ASCII figures (Necker cube labels at 5.5423, the eye diagram at 5.6331,
   the line figure at 6.36111) against page images of the 1922 edition.
2. German quotations against an online critical text, since PDF extraction loses diacritics.
3. The count of 526 propositions and the per-section counts.

### The essay

`Istoria_Logikis_kai_AI.docx` was out of scope for tiers A–E. It was reviewed afterwards: see "Results — the essay" at the end.

---

# Results — tiers A and D (checked 2026-11-14)

Verdicts: **confirmed** (sources agree with the analysis), **corrected** (the
analysis needs a change), **contested** (respectable sources disagree),
**unverifiable** (not settled in this pass). "Line" is the line number in
`TRACTATUS_LOGICO_PHILOSOPHICUS_ANALYSIS_1-7.md` at commit `7299538` (`1b600c8` before the history was rewritten on 2026-11-14).
Source quality is uneven: where the second source is only Wikipedia or a
bookseller's record, the row says so. The proposed actions were approved and applied on 2026-11-14 (Carnap weakened to «συγγενεύει με»; Lorentzatos left out).

No source ties Tarski's hierarchy to Russell's introduction. Say it anticipates the object-language / metalanguage distinction later developed by Carnap and Tarski. |

| Carroll, *Mind* 4 (1895), 278–280; Pears, *The False Prison* I (1987); Janik & Toulmin (Simon & Schuster 1973); Mauthner (Stuttgart 1901–02); Hertz (Leipzig 1894); *Principia* (CUP 1910–13) | searched, no usable record returned | **unverifiable** in this pass | none; no reason to doubt them, but they stay under the "from memory" caveat |
| Frege *Begriffsschrift* / *Grundgesetze*, Kant, Schopenhauer, Leibniz, Spinoza, Hume, Bradley (years only) | not searched | not checked | none (standard publication years) |

## Summary

- Tier A: 21 rows. 10 confirmed (three of them with small wording fixes: A2, A5, A13), 9 corrected (A4, A7, A9, A10, A11, A16, A17, A18, A19b), 2 unverifiable in this pass (A19a Carnap, A19c).
- The four claims the PDFs could not settle: Reclam **confirmed**; 1914 donation **confirmed**; Hermine's phrase **confirmed**; Sraffa sourcing **corrected**.
- Tier D: one wrong DOI (Stern), one unsupported date (Tolstoy), eight entries to complete, six entries plus the Stenius imprint still unverified, and the role of Lorentzatos in the Greek edition open.

# Results — tiers B, C and E (checked 2026-11-14)

Same verdict vocabulary as above. For interpretive claims, "contested" means
respectable sources disagree, and the action is an «Ερμηνευτική σημείωση» that
names both readings rather than picking one. All actions in this section have
been applied to the Markdown.

# Results — the essay (checked 2026-11-14)

`Istoria_Logikis_kai_AI.docx` was read in full and its claims checked online.
It was not edited. The corrected and extended text is
`Logiki_Glossa_Michanes.md` (source) and `Logiki_Glossa_Michanes.docx`
(generated by `tools/build_docx.py`). Same verdict vocabulary as above.
"SEP" is the Stanford Encyclopedia of Philosophy
(`https://plato.stanford.edu/entries/<name>/`), "MacTutor" the St Andrews
archive (`https://mathshistory.st-andrews.ac.uk/Biographies/<name>/`).
"Snippet" means the fact was seen only in a search result, not on a page that
was opened.

## Claims of the original essay

| # | Claim in the original | Sources | Verdict | Action in the new text |
| :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- |
| S1 | Chrysippus "father of propositional logic" | SEP `logic-ancient` | confirmed in substance; the label is the essay's | Label dropped; Philo's and Diodorus's conditional and the *lekta* added. |
| S2 | Boole's "+" was an exclusive or | SEP `boole`, `algebra-logic-tradition`; *Laws of Thought* (Project Gutenberg #15114) | **corrected** | "+" was defined only for classes with no common member; inclusive "+" from Jevons (1864) and Peirce (1867). |
| S3 | x² = x "proves that logic has two truth values" | same | **corrected** | Boole notes that only 0 and 1 satisfy it and derives x(1 − x) = 0, the principle of contradiction; he speaks of classes, not truth values. |
| S4 | Frege (1879) introduced the quantifiers ∀ and ∃ | SEP `frege`, `pm-notation`; MacTutor, Earliest Uses of Symbols of Set Theory and Logic | **corrected** | Frege had his own notation; ∃ is Peano's (1897), ∀ Gentzen's (1935). |
| S5 | *Wertverläufe* glossed as "extensions of concepts", placed in 1879 | SEP `frege` | **corrected** | The term is left out; Basic Law V is placed in the *Grundgesetze* (1893/1903). |
| S6 | Russell wrote to Frege in 1901 | SEP `russell-paradox`, `frege` | **corrected** | Paradox found in spring 1901; letter of 16 June 1902. Frege's appendix: "my law V … is false" (as quoted by SEP). |
| S7 | *Principia* "established" ∨ and ⊃ | SEP `principia-mathematica`, `pm-notation` | corrected in wording | The notation owes much to Peano; *Principia* made the signs familiar. |
| S8 | ZFC by Zermelo and Fraenkel | SEP `zermelo-set-theory` | confirmed; incomplete | Zermelo 1908; Fraenkel and Skolem 1922. |
| S9 | Wittgenstein visited Frege in 1911; Russell's "most exciting intellectual adventures" | SEP `wittgenstein`; MacTutor `Wittgenstein` | confirmed; the Russell sentence was seen only as quoted by MacTutor | Kept, without quotation marks. Its original place (probably Russell's obituary note in *Mind*, 1951) was not seen. |
| S10 | Truth tables "established" by Wittgenstein and Post | row A17 above | corrected as in A17 | Peirce's manuscripts mentioned. |
| S11 | Tractatus says almost nothing of Boole | the two PDFs in the folder (no occurrence of "Boole") | confirmed | «δεν αναφέρει πουθενά τον Boole». |
| S12 | Hilbert posed the *Entscheidungsproblem* "after Gödel's blow" | SEP `church-turing` | **corrected** | Hilbert & Ackermann, 1928. Church published the negative answer a few months before Turing. |
| S13 | Gödel's sentence is "an equation"; first theorem assumes consistency | SEP `goedel-incompleteness` | corrected in wording | A statement of arithmetic; Gödel used ω-consistency, Rosser (1936) reduced it to consistency. SEP's warning that "G says it is unprovable" is to be taken loosely is reflected («χαλαρά μιλώντας»). |
| S14 | Hilbert believed every mathematical truth provable | SEP `hilbert-program`; MacTutor `Hilbert` | confirmed if put as completeness and solvability | Reworded; 1930 "Wir müssen wissen, wir werden wissen". |
| S15 | Wittgenstein "rejected" Gödel's theorem | SEP `wittgenstein-mathematics`; [Lampert, WAB](https://wab.uib.no/ojs/index.php/agora-alws/article/view/2593/2910) | **contested** | «Ερμηνευτική σημείωση»: Gödel, Kreisel, Bernays versus Floyd & Putnam (2000); Bays and Rodych against the latter. Floyd & Putnam and Bays were read only through other authors. The original's football-goal analogy was dropped. |
| S16 | Turing was 24 in 1936 and "proved the halting problem" | SEP `church-turing`, `turing-machine`; Turing's paper; [S. Lucas, "The origins of the halting problem" (2021)](https://riunet.upv.es/handle/10251/189460) | **corrected** | Aged 23 at submission (May 1936). The paper proves that being "circle-free", and printing a given symbol, are undecidable; name and usual form of the halting problem from Davis (1950s); the short proof from Strachey (1965; snippet). |
| S17 | The universal machine is the basis of the modern computer | SEP `computing-history` and `turing-machine` (they differ) | **contested** | Both views stated. |
| S18 | Shannon revived a "forgotten" Boolean algebra in 1937, aged 21 | MacTutor `Shannon`; [MIT DSpace](https://dspace.mit.edu/handle/1721.1/11173) | date and age confirmed; "forgotten" **corrected** | «σχεδόν άγνωστη στους μηχανικούς»; Nakashima 1935–36 mentioned (snippet, [IPSJ museum](https://museum.ipsj.or.jp/en/computer/dawn/0002.html)). |
| S19 | Symbolic AI 1950–1980, "millions of rules", triumph in chess | SEP `artificial-intelligence`; [IBM, Deep Blue](https://www.ibm.com/history/deep-blue); Buchanan & Shortliffe 1984 (snippet) | **corrected** | Dartmouth 1956; MYCIN had some hundreds of rules; Deep Blue (1997) worked by search. "GOFAI" from Haugeland 1985 (weak source). |
| S20 | LLMs "vindicate" the later Wittgenstein | [Bender & Koller, ACL 2020](https://aclanthology.org/2020.acl-main.463.pdf); [Molino & Tagliabue, arXiv 2302.01570](https://arxiv.org/abs/2302.01570) (abstract) | **contested** | «Ερμηνευτική σημείωση». Firth's 1957 phrase and Masterman's unit rest partly on snippets. |
| S21 | LLMs are "subject to Gödel's theorems" | SEP `goedel-incompleteness`, `computational-mind`; [IEP, Lucas–Penrose argument](https://iep.utm.edu/lp-argue/) | **corrected** | Limits of computability apply to any program; Gödel's theorems concern axiom systems; Lucas–Penrose and the standard reply; Gödel's 1951 disjunction. |
| S22 | Neuro-symbolic AI is "the cutting edge" | Garcez & Lamb, *Artificial Intelligence Review* 56 (2023), doi 10.1007/s10462-023-10448-w | corrected in wording | «Μια ενεργή κατεύθυνση»; its advocates call it the next wave. |
| S23 | *Investigations*: "meaning is use", "illnesses of language" | German text of the Ludwig Wittgenstein Project | §43 corrected as in A16; "illnesses of language" **not in the text** | §43 with its qualifier; §§23, 38, 66–67, 108, 109, 255 and the preface (Sraffa) checked against the German text. |

## The owner's note `Wittgenstein intro.md` (checked 2026-11-14)

The note was read, its claims checked, and what held up was merged into the
essay (sections 6, 8 and 10). The note itself was not edited.

| # | Claim in the note | Sources | Verdict | Action in the essay |
| :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- |
| N1 | The *Tractatus* is the only book he published in his lifetime | [IEP, Wittgenstein](https://iep.utm.edu/wittgens/) ("the only philosophy book"); SEP `wittgenstein` (cites the 1929 paper); [Austria-Forum](https://austria-forum.org/af/AEIOU/Wittgenstein%2C_Ludwig/Wittgenstein%2C_Ludwig_english) (dictionary 1926) | **corrected** | «το μόνο φιλοσοφικό βιβλίο»; the 1926 school dictionary and the 1929 article named. |
| N2 | Russell was deeply influenced by his pupil | SEP `russell`, `wittgenstein-atomism`, `principia-mathematica`, `logical-atomism`; Russell's 1918 acknowledgement as quoted by SEP and by Landini (CUP) | confirmed, with a dissent | Added to section 6, with Landini's view that Russell "embellished matters" for his friend's sake. The 1918 text itself was not opened. |
| N3 | Picture theory: a proposition has sense only if testable, or if a tautology | SEP `wittgenstein`; Tractatus 4.003, 4.461, 4.4611 (LWP German text) | **corrected** | The note mixes the *Tractatus* with the Vienna Circle's verificationism, which the essay keeps apart. Tautologies are *sinnlos* but not *unsinnig*; added to section 6, using the analysis's terms («χωρίς νόημα» / «ανόητες»). The essay's rendering of 6.54 and the resolute-reading note were aligned with this. |
| N4 | Influenced philosophy of mind: Dennett, Fodor | [Dennett, *TIME* 100 (1999)](https://time.com/archive/6735017/ludwig-wittgenstein-philosopher/); [Dennett, autobiography, *Philosophy Now* 68](https://philosophynow.org/issues/68/Daniel_Dennett_Autobiography_Part_1); SEP `private-language` (Fodor 1975, pp. 68–73) | Dennett confirmed; Fodor **corrected** (a critic) | Both added, Fodor as a critic. Dennett's two sources are his own words but not peer-reviewed. |
| N5 | Turing attended the 1939 lectures and argued with Wittgenstein | SEP `turing` (Diamond 1976); [Matthíasson, *BJHP* 29 (2021)](https://research-portal.st-andrews.ac.uk/en/publications/contradictions-and-falling-bridges-what-was-wittgensteins-reply-t/) (abstract); [Floyd, BU slides (2012)](https://www.bu.edu/cphs/files/2013/08/Floyd.pdf) | confirmed; influence ran both ways | New subsection at the end of section 8: the falling-bridge exchange; Turing's 1944–45 acknowledgement; RPP I §1096 ("These machines are humans who calculate"). Diamond's edition and Turing's typescript were not opened; Floyd's are slides, not a paper. |
| N6 | He "proved" that a private language is impossible | SEP `private-language`; *Investigations* §§243, 258, 293 (LWP German text) | **contested**; "proved" too strong | Added to section 10 with the diary and beetle examples and an «Ερμηνευτική σημείωση». |
| N7 | The fly and the fly-bottle | *Investigations* §309 (LWP German text) | confirmed | Added to the passage on therapy. The toolbox (§11), also checked, added to meaning as use. |
| N8 | He showed Austin that language does things | SEP `austin-jl`; [Harris & Unnsteinsson, *BJHP* 2018](https://opinvisindi.is/handle/20.500.11815/1981) (abstract); [Austin, *How to Do Things with Words* (1962), p. 5](https://web.english.upenn.edu/~cavitch/pdf-library/Austin_How_To_Do_Things_with_Words.pdf) | **contested** | Austin's performatives added with his own examples (ship, bet; not "I do", which Austin's editor flags as a slip); «Ερμηνευτική σημείωση» on the dispute over influence. |
| N9 | "Forms of life" gave the social sciences a non-judging tool (Winch) | SEP `relativism` | confirmed; charge of relativism live | Winch's 1964 Azande article and Lukes's reply added. "Western positivist standards" is the note's own framing and was not kept. Winch's own reply to the charge was not read. |
| N10 | The only 20th-century thinker who set off two revolutions | SEP `wittgenstein`; IEP | **unverifiable** as worded | Not used. SEP's "two commonly recognized stages" and its remark that the division "has been questioned" are reflected in the new comparison table's caption. |
| N11 | Returned to philosophy in the 1930s | SEP `wittgenstein`; IEP | **corrected** | 1929 (the essay already had it). |
| N12 | Comparison table of the two phases | — | two cells **corrected** | Kept as a table. "Correspondence with physical facts" became the shared logical form of proposition and possible situation; "delimiting the scientifically sayable" became clarification and the limit between saying and showing. |

The design proposal by Gemini 4 Argon (local file, deleted after this
check) was read in full; its claims about the text were checked against the
German of the Ludwig Wittgenstein Project
([Philosophische_Untersuchungen](https://www.wittgensteinproject.org/w/index.php/Philosophische_Untersuchungen),
§§1–446 and §§600–693 read verbatim). Nothing from the proposal is in the
published texts yet; the corrections are for the coming analysis.

| :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- |

| §243 contrasts the private language with a cipher diary "like Pepys's" | §243 (LWP) | **corrected**: Pepys and any cipher are absent | The diary is in §258 (*Tagebuch*). Pepys is a commentators' example, not Wittgenstein's. |
| §79: a critique of "rigid" Russellian descriptions | §79 (LWP): «Nach Russell können wir sagen: der Name »Moses« kann durch verschiedene Beschreibungen definiert werden» | **corrected**: "rigid" is not in §79 | §79 is about descriptions for the name "Moses". "Rigid" is Kripke's later term. |
| §246: "Only I can know whether I am really in pain" is grammatical nonsense | §246 (LWP): «Das ist in einer Weise falsch, in einer andern unsinnig» | **corrected** | False in one sense, nonsense in another. |
| "Super-rigid machine" (*überstarre Maschine*), §§193–194 | §194 «die ideal starren Maschine»; §197 «über-starre Verbindung» (LWP) | **corrected** | Cite §194 and §197 with their own wording. |
| *Kristallreinheit*, §97 and §107 | §97 «vom reinsten Kristall»; §107 «Die Kristallreinheit der Logik»; §108 «Das *Vorurteil* der Kristallreinheit» (LWP) | **corrected** (§97) | The word is in §107–108; §97 has "of the purest crystal". |
| §339: thinking is not an incorporeal process | §339 (LWP): «Denken ist kein unkörperlicher Vorgang» | confirmed | none |
| The 1945 preface as Wittgenstein's "instruction" to read the books together | Vorwort (LWP): «Da schien es mir plötzlich, daß ich jene alten Gedanken und die neuen zusammen veröffentlichen sollte» | **corrected**: a wish he once had, not an instruction | Describe it as what seemed right to him at one time. |
| §39 "Excalibur" | §39 (LWP): *Nothung* | **corrected** | "Excalibur" is Anscombe's rendering; use *Nothung*. |
| Kripke "stops at the first paragraph of §201", stated as fact | Baker & Hacker (1984), McGinn (1984), McDowell (1984) argue it; Kripke (1982) presents his reading as "Wittgenstein's argument as it struck Kripke" | contested; the proposal picks a winner | An «Ερμηνευτική σημείωση» that names both readings (arc 6). Sources to be read then. |
| Part II / *PPF* xi: duck-rabbit, «halb Seherlebnis, halb ein Denken» | not in the LWP text (no Part II) | unverifiable for now | Needs another German source. |
| *Über Gewissheit* §115, §122, §§96–99, §§341–343 | LWP page missing | unverifiable for now | Open check. |
| Copyright: German PD in the EU since 2022; 1953 edition under US copyright to 2049 (95 years); Anscombe, Hacker–Schulte and Christodoulidis translations under copyright | Wittgenstein died 29 April 1951 (life + 70); US term for works of 1953 is 95 years from publication | confirmed in outline | `LICENSE` §3 rewritten: German quotations of the *Investigations* are not PD in the US. Quote key sentences only, not 60–80 whole remarks as proposed. |
| Labels for every remark: "interlocutor" or "therapeutic" voice | — | contested in itself: who speaks is an interpretive question | Not adopted as labels; voice discussed in arc 1 with a note. |

A review of the essay by Google Gemini (a PDF of the conversation and a
revised `.docx`, in the untracked folder `gemini/`) was read in full; the
`.docx` was diffed paragraph by paragraph against the current Word file
(27 change blocks, none beyond what Gemini announced). Fourteen proposals
were taken one by one with the owner (decisions in `DONE.md`).

Gemini's replacement texts, not adopted, contained these errors: the
Byzantine paragraph «με σχολιαστές όπως τον Μιχαήλ Ψελλό» (case) and the
loss of «στα *Αναλυτικά Ύστερα*»; in its §10 condensation the two Rosch
papers of 1975 merged, Brandom's «δίνουν και ζητούν λόγους» attributed to
Eagleton too, the Austin «Ερμηνευτική σημείωση» reduced to a parenthesis,
and the table cell «οι κοινωνικοί επιστήμονες» left without Winch; in the
Cammarata paragraph «κάτω από τους νευρώνες» (superposition spreads
features across neurons); in the 2026 paragraph «συνέβαλαν» for the
autonomous Erdős disproof and «ταχύτατα».

The examples 0, 1, 3 and about 6,6 bits are log₂(1/p), the standard exposition (Shannon defines the average, the entropy); the text says «το μέτρο του Shannon στηρίζεται σε μια ιδέα», not that he defined it so. |
| Γ7 | Chomsky's statistical model was of Shannon's kind (section 11) | *Syntactic Structures* (1957), §2.4 ("in any statistical model for grammaticalness") and ch. 3, which cites Shannon and Weaver (1949) for the finite-state Markov model and rejects it "for the purposes of grammar"; [Pereira 2000](https://people.cs.umass.edu/~brenocon/anlp2018/papers/Pereira2000.pdf), abstract: "formal linguistics in the Chomsky tradition, and information theory in the Shannon tradition" | confirmed | «ένα στατιστικό μοντέλο, όπως εκείνα του Shannon (ενότητα 9)». Pereira's objection is already the next sentence of the essay, so no new note. |
| Γ8 | Von Neumann, *First Draft of a Report on the EDVAC*, 30 June 1945: central arithmetic and control parts, one memory for numbers and orders; circulated under his name alone; the name «αρχιτεκτονική von Neumann» disputed (section 9) | The typescript (Moore School, «June 30, 1945»; §§2.2–2.5, 12.2 «Numbers and orders»); [SEP, «The Modern History of Computing»](https://plato.stanford.edu/entries/computing-history/): the draft set out "the ENIAC group's ideas", "von Neumann machines" "historically inappropriate"; Computer History Museum, «The Neverending Quest for Firsts»: credit not shared with Eckert and Mauchly | confirmed | Paragraph, glossary entry, chronology row, source entry. SEP dates the composition May 1945; the document's date is kept. |
| Γ9 | Around 1900 Russell and Moore turned against the idealism of Bradley and others, which drew on Hegel and dominated British philosophy (section 6) | [SEP, Russell](https://plato.stanford.edu/entries/russell/): "towards the end of 1898 … Moore and I rebelled against both Kant and Hegel"; [IEP, Analytic Philosophy](https://iep.utm.edu/analytic-philosophy/): the break with "Absolute Idealism", "the dominant school"; [SEP, Bradley](https://plato.stanford.edu/entries/bradley/): "Neo-Hegelian" a label with "significant degree of distortion"; [SEP, Moore](https://plato.stanford.edu/entries/moore/): Cambridge "one of" the centres | confirmed; the first wording **corrected** | «που αντλούσε από τον Hegel» kept (not «εγελιανοί»). «από αυτή την αντίδραση γεννήθηκε η αναλυτική φιλοσοφία» became «Η αντίδραση αυτή, μαζί με το έργο του Frege, θεωρείται η αφετηρία της αναλυτικής φιλοσοφίας», in line with Dummett's Frege in section 6. |
| Γ10 | G. E. Moore 1873–1958 | SEP, Moore | confirmed | Dates moved to his first mention (section 6). |
| Γ11 | Wang tiles are placed without rotation; each row of a tiling can copy one step of a program (section 8, sentence and figure) | Robinson 1971 (row "Wang 1961, Berger 1966; tilings and Turing machines" above) | confirmed | Sentence added («Η ιδέα είναι…»); figure `wang_tiles.svg`. |

## The postscript on self-improvement, checked (2026-11-19)

The owner asked for a short postscript at the end of section 12 on
recursive self-improvement and on direct editing of network weights by a
language model, ending on the question of who checks such a system. Two
research passes by a subagent (news from September 2026; weight-space
editing) were followed by reading the primary sources below; the
owner's draft was then corrected against them.

| # | Claim | Source | Verdict | Action |
| :-: | :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- |
| Υ1 | Turing, c. 1951: once machines think they will soon outstrip our "feeble powers" | Turing, «Intelligent Machinery, A Heretical Theory», in Copeland (ed.), *The Essential Turing* (2004), ch. 12: "once the machine thinking method had started, it would not take long to outstrip our feeble powers"; next: "we should have to expect the machines to take control". Copeland's introduction: a talk on the BBC radio programme *The '51 Society*, Manchester; Quote Investigator: a lecture draft, Manchester 1951 | confirmed | «γύρω στο 1951, σε μια ραδιοφωνική ομιλία του». |
| Υ2 | Good 1965: a machine far surpassing any man could design better machines; the last invention, provided it is docile enough to tell us how to keep it under control | Good, «Speculations Concerning the First Ultraintelligent Machine», *Advances in Computers* 6 (1965), p. 33, read in the scanned paper | confirmed | The control proviso quoted in Greek. |
| Υ3 | Good worked with Turing at Bletchley Park | [MacTutor, Good](https://mathshistory.st-andrews.ac.uk/Biographies/Good/): "At first Good worked in Hut 8 headed by Alan Turing" (from May 1941) | confirmed | «συνεργάτης του στο Bletchley Park». |
| Υ4 | Over 80% of the code is written by the model | [Anthropic, «When AI builds itself»](https://www.anthropic.com/institute/recursive-self-improvement) (update 18 Sept. 2026): "As of May 2026, more than 80% of the code we merge into Anthropic's codebase was authored by Claude"; no other company's figure on the page | confirmed for Anthropic only; the draft **corrected** | «Σύμφωνα με τις ίδιες τις εταιρείες … του κώδικα με τον οποίο εκπαιδεύονται οι διάδοχοί τους» → «Σύμφωνα με την Anthropic … του κώδικα που ενσωματώνει η εταιρεία». The page also: "We are not there yet, and recursive self-improvement is not inevitable." |
| Υ5 | Anthropic is approaching its threshold for «δραματικά επιταχυνόμενη πρόοδο» | S-1/A Amendment No. 1 as quoted by [TechCrunch, 2 Nov. 2026](https://techcrunch.com/2026/11/02/anthropic-warns-that-its-very-close-to-rsi-capability-aka-self-building-ai/): "internal estimates and our expectations regarding future models, rather than our current models, indicate that we may imminently meet our Responsible Scaling Policy's definition of 'dramatically accelerated progress attributable to AI'" | confirmed through the quotation; the filing itself **not read** | «πλησιάζει … με βάση όσα περιμένει από τα επόμενα μοντέλα της». The draft's «αν δεν έχει ήδη φτάσει» (Amendment No. 2) removed: EDGAR refuses requests from undeclared automated tools, and the owner's address was not used. |
| Υ6 | METR: about twice the earlier rate on well-specified tasks, mostly due to AI | [METR, 5 Nov. 2026](https://metr.org/blog/2026-11-05-anthropic-ai-rd-acceleration-assessment/): "at around twice the rate they did between March 2024 and October 2025", 1.9× (95% CI 1.5–2.8×); "perhaps ≈80%" due to AI, an "overall subjective estimate"; "Anthropic redacted a majority of the evidence we found most informative"; no sampling access to non-public models; compute verification cancelled | confirmed | «κυρίως, κατά την εκτίμησή της». |
| Υ7 | MIT researchers: a large language model read and directly edited the weights of a smaller one, without training | [Takehi, Teoh, Zhang, Khattab (MIT CSAIL), 2 Nov. 2026](https://rikiyat.github.io/blog/weight-space-programming/): GPT-6 Astra edited Qwen3.5-0.8B "with no training", 131,072 weights; MATH-500 7.9% → 48.1%; "could not add an ability the model lacked"; "not a method"; a blog post, no arXiv version. [Willison, 3 Nov.](https://simonwillison.net/2026/Nov/3/qwen-can-interpret-model-weights/) repeated the read-only test only | confirmed; not reviewed | «με αποτελέσματα που δεν έχουν ακόμη κριθεί από άλλους». |

Left out: the Gladstone report (anonymous insiders, via TIME), OpenAI's
"research intern" page (403; secondary reports only), Anthropic's
Amendment No. 2 (not read). The facts of the paragraph rest twice on
Anthropic's own statements and once on METR's assessment of Anthropic;
noted to the owner.

## The «Διάλεξη για την Ηθική» and Κιντή's paper, read for 6.4 – 6.54 (2026-11-22)

Method: the lecture read in full in English and in the Greek translation
(wikitext of both LWP pages through `api.php`); Κιντή's paper read in full
(`pdftotext` of the local, git-ignored PDF); nine proposals taken through
with the owner one by one, in text order. Quotations of the lecture in the
analysis are our own translations from the English.

| # | Claim / location | Source | Verdict | Action |
| :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- |
| Φ1 | The lecture: the world-book and the one level of all propositions (6.4); relative and absolute value, the chair, the road, tennis and the lie (6.41); the book that would destroy all other books, the teacup, the "absolutely right road" and the "coercive power of an absolute judge" (6.42, 6.422); wonder at the existence of the world, the sky, the tautology, the world seen as a miracle (6.44, 6.45); running against the boundaries of language, the cage, the tendency he would not ridicule (7) | [Lecture on Ethics, LWP](https://www.wittgensteinproject.org/w/index.php/Lecture_on_Ethics) (text of *The Philosophical Review* 74, 1965, pp. 3–12); [Greek translation by Ι. Μπαρτσίδη, LWP, 2022](https://www.wittgensteinproject.org/w/index.php/%CE%9C%CE%B9%CE%B1_%CE%B4%CE%B9%CE%AC%CE%BB%CE%B5%CE%BE%CE%B7_%CE%B3%CE%B9%CE%B1_%CF%84%CE%B7%CE%BD_%CE%97%CE%B8%CE%B9%CE%BA%CE%AE) | confirmed; the links from the lecture to single propositions are our reading and are worded as such («Με αυτή τη διάκριση διαβάζεται», «Έτσι διαβάζεται», «Αυτό ταιριάζει με») | Five paragraphs added (after 6.4, after 6.41, after the group 6.42, 6.422–6.423, after 6.45, in Proposition 7). The Greek translation has «κοινές» for "trivial" (ours: «ασήμαντες») and «κελιού» for "cage" (kept, owner). It omits two sentences of the English on the dog and on "has only sense if I can imagine it not to be the case". |
| Φ2 | The «Παράδειγμα» on the murder: only physical details; the wrongness of the murder «κείτεται στο όριο του κόσμου» | The lecture: "with all its details physical and psychological"; the pain and rage of those who hear of it are "facts, facts, and facts but no Ethics"; nothing on a limit of the world | **corrected** | Rewritten: the psychological details and the sentence on pain and rage added; the gloss on the limit of the world removed; the last sentence marked as ours and pointed to 6.41 (owner's wording); title «Διάλεξη για την Ηθική» throughout. Row A12 stands. |
| Φ3 | Proposition 7: theologians and moral philosophers cheapen ethics into «κούφια φλυαρία (*Geschwätz*)» | Waismann, "Notes on Talks with Wittgenstein", *The Philosophical Review* 74 (1965), pp. 12–13, [scan](https://cla.csulb.edu/departments/philosophy/wp-content/uploads/2025/12/Waismann-1965-Notes-on-talks-with-Wittgenstein.pdf) read by OCR: «Ich halte es für sicher wichtig, daß man all dem Geschwätz über Ethik — ob es eine Erkenntnis gebe, ob es Werte gebe, ob sich das Gute definieren lasse etc. — ein Ende macht» (Monday 30 December 1929, at Schlick's); the lecture's closing lines | **corrected**: the word is his, but for the theory of ethics (Moore's questions), not for religious or moral speech; the lecture says he would not ridicule the tendency | The clause on theologians and moral philosophers and «κούφια» removed; the word kept with its date and source (owner). The same note names Kierkegaard for the "running against the limits of language" and opens with «das Erstaunen, daß etwas existiert». |
| Φ4 | Engelmann: the positivists "have nothing to be silent about"; the coastline of the island and the boundary of the ocean | Κιντή (2023), p. 61, quoting Engelmann, *Letters from Ludwig Wittgenstein with a Memoir* (1967), p. 97, in her Greek | read only through Κιντή; the English not reached | In Proposition 7, as a paraphrase with one short quotation. Open in `CROSSCHECK_PLAN.md`. |
| Φ5 | Κιντή, «Η σκάλα του *Tractatus*», *Δευκαλίων* 37/1-2 (2023), pp. 50–64 | [Her CV of September 2023](https://www.phs.uoa.gr/fileadmin/depts/phs.uoa.gr/www/uploads/cv/kinti.pdf) («2023 υπό δημοσίευση … Δευκαλίων»); the page numbers of the PDF; issue number from [her English CV of 2025](https://en.phs.uoa.gr/fileadmin/depts/phs.uoa.gr/www/uploads/cv/CV_Kindi__English_2025.pdf), read by the owner | confirmed | Bibliography Δ΄. |

| Φ7 | Hacker (2015): 6.54 read with 4.1213; the book a tree, after Bazzocchi; the right view from the "ground floor"; the ladder only an image for discarding. Κιντή: the ladder is an ascent, tied to 6.45 | Κιντή (2023), pp. 58–59; that Hacker (2015) reads the book as a tree: [Kraft, *Nordic Wittgenstein Review*, 2016](https://nordicwittgensteinreview.com/article/download/3415/4105) (search summary) | **contested**; Hacker's article not reached, so the "ground floor" and 4.1213 rest on Κιντή | Second «Ερμηνευτική σημείωση» after the one on 6.54, naming both readings, with «όπως τον παρουσιάζει η Κιντή». |
| Φ8 | Wittgenstein, 1930: a place reachable only by a ladder does not interest him | Κιντή (2023), p. 59, quoting *Πολιτισμός και αξίες*, p. 25; [Gakis, "Throwing Away the Ladder Before Climbing it", ALWS 2010](https://wab.uib.no/ojs/index.php/agora-alws/article/view/2891) | confirmed | In the new note, as a paraphrase, with both ways of taking it. |
| Φ9 | Ramsey: "what we can't say we can't say, and we can't whistle it either" | Κιντή (2023), p. 50 (Ramsey 1931, p. 238); manuscript dated 1929, printed by Braithwaite in 1931: [Cambridge repository](https://www.repository.cam.ac.uk/handle/1810/194722) | confirmed for the dates; the wording and the page through Κιντή | Point 3 of the dialogue; bibliography Δ΄. |
| Φ10 | An outside summary of Κιντή's paper, pasted by the owner | The paper | accurate in outline. It flattens her conclusion to scaffolding alone (she holds both scaffolding and ascent), places the «ίλιγγος» of the *Theaetetus* at the sight of the Forms, and gives the *via negativa* as hers (she reports Taylor and Nieli) | Not used as a source. |

## Outside readings compared with the analysis (2026-11-22)

Method: follow-up 7 of the review of 2026-11-21. The IEP article and six
pages of wittgenstein.be were read and their checkable claims tested against
the Markdown by search; each difference was taken to a better source before
any change. SparkNotes refused every automated request (HTTP 403); it was read later the
same day through the owner's browser (Χ9–Χ12).
readtractatus.com is a script-driven viewer of the text, one proposition per
slide, with no commentary in the page source: nothing to compare. No claim of
the two sites read contradicts the analysis; the rows below are what they
state and the analysis did not. Five proposals taken through with the owner
one by one, in text order.

| # | Claim / location | Source | Verdict | Action |
| :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- |
| Χ1 | The standing of the two sites | [IEP, "Ludwig Wittgenstein"](https://iep.utm.edu/wittgens/) (Duncan J. Richter, Virginia Military Institute); [wittgenstein.be, About](https://wittgenstein.be/en/about/) | the IEP is a reference work with a named author; wittgenstein.be names no author and gives only the SEP, Gutenberg and the Bergen edition as sources | The IEP used as a source (Χ5). wittgenstein.be used only as a prompt: nothing rests on it. |
| Χ2 | What the two sites state and the analysis already had: 1921 Ostwald and 1922 Kegan Paul; Ogden with Ramsey, checked by Wittgenstein; the Latin title from Moore after Spinoza; Pinsent; the Paris court model, 29.9.1914, with von Wright's testimony; the letter to Ficker of 1919; Ramsey on whistling; the traditional and resolute readings of 6.54; Engelmann on the positivists; 4.0312, 4.112, 4.121, 4.1212, 5.43, 5.6, 5.62, 6.44, 6.522, 7 as quoted | The Markdown, by search | agree | No change. |
| Χ3 | What the objects are (2.02): particulars only (Copi 1958; Anscombe 1959) or properties and relations too (Stenius 1960, pp. 61–69; Hintikka and Hintikka 1986, pp. 30–34), with 2.0231 and 3.1432, a *Notebooks* entry of 1915 (NB, p. 61) and Lee's notes of 1930–31 on 2.01; visual-field patches tried in June 1915 (NB, p. 64) and "short-lived"; "Perhaps Wittgenstein simply did not have an antecedent opinion"; no a priori list of the forms of elementary propositions (4.221, 5.553–5.5541, 5.5571); the remark to Malcolm (1989 edition, p. 70) | [SEP, "Wittgenstein's Logical Atomism"](https://plato.stanford.edu/entries/wittgenstein-atomism/) (Ian Proops; 2004, revised 13 Sep 2022), sections 1 and 4, read in full. (d) the operation sign Ω of 6.02 is printed as "1/2". Point 4 not pursued; point 5 left for the *Investigations* part. |
| Χ13 | Note at 2.02, third question: whether the objects come before language. Realist reading (Malcolm 1986; Pears 1987; Hacker 1986): the object's nature governs the name's use. Against it (Ishiguro 1969; McGuinness 1981): the reference of a name is not secured independently of its use in propositions. Both sides accept 3.3 | M. L. Engelmann, *Reading Wittgenstein's Tractatus* (Cambridge Elements, 2021), §1.1, publisher's excerpt: "realist readings misleadingly presuppose that 'the meaning of a name can be secured independently of its use in propositions' (Ishiguro, 1969: 20)"; "what fixes use is the object named" (on Malcolm 1986: 29). M. Bartmann, *Wittgenstein's Metametaphysics and the Realism-Idealism Debate* (2021), ch. 3, notes 126, 138, 145, 146, quoting Pears 1987, p. 111: "in the same general way as Ishiguro and McGuinness, but reverses the direction of fit". C. Johnston (2017) for Malcolm, Pears and Hacker. [SEP, "Ludwig Wittgenstein"](https://plato.stanford.edu/entries/wittgenstein/), §2.4, states the dispute without names | **confirmed**, through others: none of the five authors was read directly. SparkNotes's wording (objects "given being only in the context of states of affairs") was not adopted; the sources put the difference as one about reference. Not taken up: Johnston's distinction of Hacker from Pears and Malcolm; Nakano's criticism of Ishiguro (*Analysis* 81, 2021) | Added; five entries in Δ΄. |

Black: perhaps "world" and "total reality" are synonyms, the second stressing the negative side; he refers to 1.12 | R. J. Fogelin, *Wittgenstein*, 2nd ed. (1987), p. 13: "I do not think that there is any way to restore perfect terminological consistency to the text, but … this slip is quite natural and, in the end, innocent of deep systematic importance". M. Black, *Companion* (1964), p. 70: "In the light of 2.05, perhaps the right answer is that 'the world' and 'total reality' are synonyms". Both read in the OCR text of archive.org scans | **contested** between the two; both readings named. Page numbers are inferred from the OCR page markers, not seen on the page. Not taken up: Bartmann (2021, n. 11), who holds that the world includes the non-existing states of affairs, seen only in fragments. Anscombe does not discuss the point; Stenius could not be read | Added. |
| Χ15 | Note at 6.02 – 6.03: Frege defined in purely logical terms what it is to follow in a series (*Begriffsschrift*, Part III) so that arithmetic need not rest on intuition, and defined the natural numbers with it; *Principia* follows him; Wittgenstein charges a vicious circle (4.1273) and takes repeated application as given (5.2523, 6.233). Russell: only finite numbers. Ramsey: cannot cover the whole of mathematics | [SEP, "Frege's Theorem"](https://plato.stanford.edu/entries/frege-theorem/), §4.2 and §6.1 ("no appeal to intuition is required"); [SEP, "Logicism and Neologicism"](https://plato.stanford.edu/entries/logicism/), §1.2.4; [SEP, "Principia Mathematica"](https://plato.stanford.edu/entries/principia-mathematica/) ("This follows Frege's definition", at \*90·01); [SEP, "Wittgenstein's Philosophy of Mathematics"](https://plato.stanford.edu/entries/wittgenstein-mathematics/), §1. Russell's Introduction: "his theory of number (6.02 ff.) which, as it stands, is only capable of dealing with finite numbers". Ramsey's notice of 1923 in the 1931 volume, p. 282: "I do not see how this account can be supposed to cover the whole of mathematics, and it is evidently incomplete since there are also inequalities" | **confirmed**. SparkNotes's claim that Wittgenstein was "strangely blind" to the difficulty of "and so on" was **not adopted**: no source read criticises the "and so on" itself, and the charge of circularity is Wittgenstein's against Frege and Russell. "Frege sought to eliminate 'and so on'" is not in any source; the note says what he defined and why. Not taken up: Ramsey's "ridiculously narrow view of mathematics" (1925, p. 17 of the 1931 volume), which concerns equations; Weiss (2017) on the obscure origins of the concept of operation | Added. |

## The online checks of the essay, closed (2026-11-23)

Option A of the choice left on 2026-11-19: the essay checks that could be
done online. Wikidata was read through its API, MacTutor and the SEP as
pages, the GND through lobid.org; Britannica refuses automated requests and
was not used. Nothing in the essay changed.

| :-: | :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- |

| Ψ3 | Gödel's G built by putting into a phrase with an empty place for a number, «αν στη φράση με αριθμό x βάλουμε τον ίδιο τον αριθμό x, η πρόταση που προκύπτει δεν έχει απόδειξη», the phrase's own number; the result is true exactly when G has no proof; kinship with the liar and with Cantor's diagonal (section 7) | Gödel 1931, §1, pp. 174–176, in [Hirzel's translation (2000)](https://comp.anu.edu.au/courses/comp2310-2020/Papers/1931/Goedel/On%20formally%20undecidable%20propositions%20of.pdf): the class K of the numbers n for which R_n(n) is not provable, S = R_q, so R_q(q) "states its own unprovability"; "close kinship with the liar-antinomy"; from this "it immediately follows that R_q(q) is correct". [SEP, "Gödel's Incompleteness Theorems"](https://plato.stanford.edu/entries/goedel-incompleteness/) (Raatikainen), §2.4 and the supplement on the Diagonalization Lemma: B(x) says that the result of substituting x into the formula numbered x has the property; D is B with its own number; only a provable equivalence, not sameness of meaning; Gödel proved only this special case (provability), the general lemma is Carnap's (1934) | confirmed | None. The essay's wording, corrected on 2026-11-18, is Gödel's own sketch; «Χαλαρά μιλώντας» and «είναι αληθής ακριβώς τότε όταν» match the SEP's caution about "says of itself". The essay does not name the general lemma, so Carnap need not be added. |
| Ψ4 | Boole: in ordinary algebra only 0 and 1 satisfy x² = x, so he proposed an algebra whose symbols take only these two values; x(1 − x) = 0 gives the principle of contradiction (section 4) | *An Investigation of the Laws of Thought* (1854), read in two copies on the Internet Archive: the scan of the 1854 edition (`investigationofl00boolrich`) and the mirror of Gutenberg #15114 (`aninvestigationo15114gut`; gutenberg.org itself still returns 503). Ch. II §15, pp. 37–38: "the equation x² = x, considered as algebraic, has no other roots than 0 and 1 … Let us conceive, then, of an Algebra in which the symbols x, y, z, &c. admit indifferently of the values 0 and 1, and of these values alone." Ch. III, Prop. IV, p. 49: x − x² = 0, hence x(1 − x) = 0, "that 'principle of contradiction' which Aristotle has described as the fundamental axiom of all philosophy" | confirmed; the SEP's quotation (row Δ2) is exact | None. |

## The essay and the analysis compared (2026-11-24)

At the owner's request the essay was read in full against the whole
analysis (three readers, one third of the analysis each; every point
below then checked in both files). No contradiction on the reading of
the *Tractatus* itself. Step 1 of the follow-up (the fixes that need no
decision) was applied; the rest is in `TODO.md` and `CROSSCHECK_PLAN.md`.

## The note on *Sachverhalt* revised (2026-11-25)

| # | Item | Sources | Verdict | Action taken |
| :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- |
| S1 | The «Ερμηνευτική σημείωση» on *Sachverhalt* (possible combination or existing atomic fact; rows B1 above) gave the 1919 letter to Russell as the evidence for the second reading, without its context or the textual evidence on either side | `tlp_de.json`: 2.05, 2.06, 4.25, 4.27 (*Sachverhalte* that do not obtain); 2.0124, 2.013 («mögliche Sachverhalte»); 2. [Dožudić, *Filozofia* 76 (2021)](https://www.sav.sk/journals/uploads/10031758filozofia.2021.76.7.4.pdf), pp. 533–534: the letter of 19.8.1919 answers Russell's question of 13.8.1919 («What is the difference between Tatsache and Sachverhalt?», *Wittgenstein in Cambridge* pp. 96–97); Wittgenstein's wording to Frege, «What corresponds to an elementary proposition, if it is true, is the existence of an atomic fact», as Frege quotes it in his letter of 16.9.1919 (De Pellegrin (ed.), *Interactive Wittgenstein*, 2011, p. 59); his conclusion that «reference to existence in proposed explanations bears not too much weight». [Sullivan & Johnston (2018)](https://dspace.stir.ac.uk/bitstream/1893/23779/1/PMS-CJ%20paper%20for%20OUP%20Truth%20Handbook.pdf), §3.1: Russell's Introduction «informed by a week's intense discussion with Wittgenstein at the end of that year». SEP, Proops, «Wittgenstein's Logical Atomism» §3.1: possible atomic states of affairs that obtain in one world and not another; the letter not discussed | **confirmed**; note completed | Note rewritten (owner, 2026-11-25): the textual evidence for each reading, Russell's question, the wording sent to Frege with Dožudić's conclusion, the date of the Introduction, and the reason the table follows the first reading. Moved to follow «Προσοχή στη μεταφραστική παγίδα», before «Οι όροι στην ελληνική μετάφραση». Proposed and left out (owner): a sentence observing that the reply compares one elementary proposition with a logical product. Not confirmed, so not used: the phrase «superficiality and misunderstanding» of the 1920 letter on the Introduction. The Frege letter read only as Dožudić quotes it. Also aligned with the first reading: the example at 1.11 («3 δυνατές (ατομικές) καταστάσεις πραγμάτων» in a logical space, not «ατομικά γεγονότα» in a world) and the logical-atomism drawer in `concepts.json` (Sachverhalte that obtain or not, not «ατομικά γεγονότα» that happen or not). |

An outside review of the essay (`new_review.txt`, Greek, untracked; no revised
`.docx`) was read in full after a full read of the essay, each proposal checked
against `DONE.md` and the ledger, and the proposals taken one by one with the
owner in the order of the text. Its praise of the figures and numbers needed no
action.

## The Riemann recheck, the fourth outside review, and new names (2026-11-26)

The Riemann result rechecked after the last stage of Sutherland's checks
was due, and a short outside review of the revised essay (`final_review.md`,
Greek, untracked; deleted afterwards at the owner's request) checked point
by point against the text. The owner approved all five changes. Then the
owner's question on Knuth (Η6–Η9), searched and decided the same day, and
the owner's question on Dijkstra (Η10–Η11), Viète from the owner's draft (Η12), then Fermat and Euler (Η13–Η14), the Greek mathematicians in §1 (Η15), and the calculus with Riemann in §2 (Η16).

One «Ερμηνευτική σημείωση» for Thales and Pythagoras (van der Waerden; Huffman). Placed at the end of §1 (owner), so that Hypatia's death leads into Augustine in §2. Timeline: Euclid in the row of about 300 BC; rows for Archimedes and Diophantus. |
| Η16 | §2: Leibniz and Newton, the calculus (1684, 1686; Newton 1665–1666, published later), ∫ and dx (1675), Leibniz on notation; Cauchy and Riemann's integral (1854); Riemann's 1854 lecture on curvature; Einstein's general relativity (1915) on that geometry; Riemann and topology; the 1859 hypothesis. Glossary «Απειροστικός λογισμός» (owner's draft, checked and condensed) | [MacTutor, The rise of calculus](https://mathshistory.st-andrews.ac.uk/HistTopics/The_rise_of_calculus/): Newton's tract on fluxions, October 1666; «By 1675 Leibniz had settled on the notation ∫ y dy … written exactly as it would be today»; integral calculus «published in 1684 and 1686»; «Leibniz was very conscious that finding a good notation was of fundamental importance and thought a lot about it»; Barrow and the inverse of differentiation, Newton stated the fundamental theorem; Cavalieri, Fermat; «the really satisfactory basis … the work of Cauchy». [MacTutor, Riemann](https://mathshistory.st-andrews.ac.uk/Biographies/Riemann/): 17 Sept. 1826 – 20 July 1866; «defining what we now call the Riemann integral»; lecture of 10 June 1854, «the definition of the curvature tensor»; curvature found from «deviations from Pythagoras's theorem»; DSB quoted: «In the mathematical apparatus developed from Riemann's address, Einstein found the frame»; the thesis «introduced topological methods into complex function theory». Wikipedia: [Leibniz–Newton calculus controversy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leibniz%E2%80%93Newton_calculus_controversy) (*Nova Methodus* 1684; the *Principia*'s geometric form; Newton in print 1693 and 1704); [Method of Fluxions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Method_of_Fluxions) (finished 1671, published 1736); [Integral symbol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Integral_symbol) (1675, private writings); [Riemann integral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riemann_integral) (presented 1854, published 1868); [Riemannian geometry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riemannian_geometry) («enabled the formulation of Einstein's general theory of relativity»; four-dimensional pseudo-Riemannian manifolds); [General relativity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_relativity) (field equations, November 1915) | confirmed; corrected in two points | «δημοσίευσε … κυρίως στο *Principia* το 1687» corrected to «πολύ αργότερα» (the *Principia* geometric; fluxions in print 1693, 1704, 1736); «τον τετραδιάστατο χωρόχρονο» → a curved four-dimensional spacetime (four dimensions from Minkowski, 1908). Left out (owner): the fundamental theorem as a formula (LaTeX), the applications, the priority dispute; «καθοριστικά», «περίφημη». A paragraph in «Ο Leibniz» after the photo of his machine; Riemann's Greek form and dates moved there from the postscript («της υπόθεσης του Riemann (ενότητα 2)»). Glossary entry after «Αναλυτικές και συνθετικές κρίσεις». Timeline 1684–1686; Riemann added to the 1854 row. |

## The coherence read of the essay (2026-11-26, later)

The whole essay read for coherence, balance and missing names after the
additions of the same day. The proposals were taken one by one with the
owner. Restructuring without new claims (not rows below): §11 «Τα
νευρωνικά δίκτυα και τα γλωσσικά μοντέλα» split into «Τα νευρωνικά
δίκτυα» and «Τα γλωσσικά μοντέλα»; §10 new subsection «Βεβαιότητα και
γνώση» (On Certainty, Gettier); six long paragraphs split, wording
unchanged.

the open checks of `CROSSCHECK_PLAN.md` to be taken as far as the open
internet allows. Ν1–Ν4 are the sources list; Ν5–Ν20 the open checks; Ν21 the Riemann watch of 2026-11-27.
